%%BeginProcSet: pspictpg.pro
%!

%          % Pour deboguer et afficher des valeurs de variables
%          /chaine 20 string def
%          /nligne 0 def
%          /imprime {
%            /Times-Roman findfont 12 scalefont setfont
%            /nligne nligne 16 sub def -50 nligne moveto chaine cvs show
%          } def

% Postscrip uses TEX's bp which is 1/72 of an inch
% instead of TEX's pt which is 1/72.27 of an inch.
% Therefore, we scale the postscript.
/!BP{ 72 72.27 div dup scale } def

% return -1, 0 or 1 depending on the sign of the top of stack
/!sign {dup 0 lt {pop -1} {0 eq {0} {1} ifelse} ifelse} def

% line from (0,0) to the point (x,y) specified on the ps stack
/!psline { newpath 0 0 moveto lineto stroke } def

% ps implementation of latex \line(a,b){L}
% arguments a,b,L on the ps stack
/!pslatexline {
  /L exch def /b exch def /a exch def
  a 0 eq {0 L b !sign mul} {L a !sign mul dup b mul a div} ifelse
  !psline
} def

% head of an arrow ending at the current point and in x direction
% use rotate before to have the right direction.
% Length of the arrow on top of stack
/!arrowhead {
  dup dup
  neg exch 3 div  rlineto
  0 exch 3 div 2 mul neg rlineto
  closepath fill
} def

% The length of an arrow head is 9 times the width of the line
/!setheadlength { 9 mul } def

% vector from (0,0) to the point (x,y).
% The arrow head is of length HL.
% arguments HL,x,y on the ps stack
/!psvect {
  newpath 0 0 moveto 2 copy lineto stroke
  2 copy moveto
  2 copy 0 eq exch 0 eq and {pop pop 1 0} if
  exch atan rotate
  !arrowhead
} def

% ps implementation of latex \vector(a,b){L}
% The arrow head is of length HL.
% arguments HL,a,b,L on the ps stack
/!pslatexvector {
  /L exch def /b exch def /a exch def
  a 0 eq b 0 eq and {/a 1 def} if
  L 0 eq {
    newpath 0 0 moveto
    b a atan rotate
    !arrowhead
    }{
    a 0 eq {0 L b !sign mul} {L a !sign mul dup b mul a div} ifelse
    !psvect
    } ifelse
} def

% Circle centered on the current point
% with diameter D specified on the ps stack
/!pscircle { 2 div newpath 0 0 3 2 roll 0 360 arc stroke } def

% filled circle centered on the current point
% with diameter D specified on the ps stack
/!psdisk { 2 div newpath 0 0 3 2 roll 0 360 arc fill } def

% Cubic Bezier curve.
% HL b x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 on the stack.
% x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 are the control points of the Bezier curve.
% b is a boolean which is true if an arrowhead is requested.
% HL is only present when b is true and define the length of the arrowhead.
/!pscbezier {
  /y3 exch def /x3 exch def /y2 exch def /x2 exch def
  /y1 exch def /x1 exch def
  newpath moveto
  x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 curveto stroke
  { x3 y3 moveto
    y3 y2 sub x3 x2 sub
    2 copy 0 eq exch 0 eq and {pop pop 0 1} if
    atan rotate !arrowhead
  } if
} def

% Quadratic Bezier curve.
% HL b x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 on the stack.
% x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 are the control points of the Bezier curve.
% b is a boolean which is true if an arrowhead is requested.
% HL is only present when b is true and define the length of the arrowhead.
%
% qbezier(P0,P1,P2) = cbezier(P0,(P0+2P1)/3,(P2+2P1)/3,P2)
/!psqbezier {
  /y2 exch def /x2 exch def  /y1 exch def /x1 exch def
  /y0 exch def /x0 exch def
  x0 y0
  x0 x1 add x1 add 3 div
  y0 y1 add y1 add 3 div
  x2 x1 add x1 add 3 div
  y2 y1 add y1 add 3 div
  x2 y2
  !pscbezier
} def

%%------------------------------------------------------------
%% The following procedures are specific to autograph.
%% They allow to draw the curves only outside the states.

% Restricted cubic Bezier curve.
% HL b r0 r3 x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 on the stack.
% x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 x3 y3 are the control points of the Bezier curve.
% The curve is only drawn outside the disks of radius r0 and r3
% centered on (x0,y0) and (x3,y3).
% b is a boolean which is true if an arrowhead is requested
% HL is only present when b is true and define the length of the arrowhead.
/!ps_r_cbezier {
  /y3 exch def /x3 exch def /y2 exch def /x2 exch def
  /y1 exch def /x1 exch def /y0 exch def /x0 exch def
  /r3 exch def /r0 exch def
%   Coefficients of the polynomials defining the Bezier curve
%   x(t)=xa.t^3+xb.t^2+xc.t+x0 and y(t)=ya.t^3+yb.t^2+yc.t+y0
  /xa x1 x2 sub 3 mul x3 x0 sub add def
  /xb x0 x1 sub x1 sub x2 add 3 mul def
  /xc x1 x0 sub 3 mul def
  /ya y1 y2 sub 3 mul y3 y0 sub add def
  /yb y0 y1 sub y1 sub y2 add 3 mul def
  /yc y1 y0 sub 3 mul def
%   First point parameter
  /t1 0 def /t2 1 def
  { /t t1 t2 add 2 div def
    t calculx x0 sub dup mul
    t calculy y0 sub dup mul add sqrt
    dup r0 sub abs 0.2 le {pop exit} if % error < 0.2pt < 0.1mm
    r0 lt { /t1 t def } { /t2 t def } ifelse
    t1 t2 eq {exit} if
  } loop
  /ta t def
%   Last point parameter
  /t1 0 def /t2 1 def
  { /t t1 t2 add 2 div def
    t calculx x3 sub dup mul
    t calculy y3 sub dup mul add sqrt
    dup r3 sub abs 0.2 le {pop exit} if % error < 0.2pt < 0.1mm
    r3 gt { /t1 t def } { /t2 t def } ifelse
    t1 t2 eq {exit} if
  } loop
  /tb t def
%   New points
  ta calculx ta calculy
  ta calculdx 3 div tb ta sub mul ta calculx add
  ta calculdy 3 div tb ta sub mul ta calculy add
  tb calculdx 3 div ta tb sub mul tb calculx add
  tb calculdy 3 div ta tb sub mul tb calculy add
  tb calculx tb calculy
% Bezier curve
  !pscbezier
} def

% We need to compute the points on the Bezier curve to find by approximation
% the parameters of the first and last points to be drawn.
% The Bezier curve is defined by
% x(t)=xa.t^3+xb.t^2+xc.t+x0 and y(t)=ya.t^3+yb.t^2+yc.t+y0
/calculx { % parameter t on the stack
  dup dup xa mul xb add mul xc add mul x0 add
} def

/calculy { % parameter t on the stack
  dup dup ya mul yb add mul yc add mul y0 add
} def

% We also need the derivatives to compute the new control points
/calculdx { % parameter t on the stack
  dup 3 xa mul mul 2 xb mul add mul xc add
} def

/calculdy { % parameter t on the stack
  dup 3 ya mul mul 2 yb mul add mul yc add
} def

% Restricted quadratic Bezier curve.
% HL b r0 r2 x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 on the stack.
% x0 y0 x1 y1 x2 y2 are the control points of the Bezier curve.
% The curve is only drawn outside the disks of radius r0 and r2
% centered on (x0,y0) and (x2,y2).
% b is a boolean which is true if an arrowhead is requested
% HL is only present when b is true and define the length of the arrowhead.
%
% qbezier(P0,P1,P2) = cbezier(P0,(P0+2P1)/3,(P2+2P1)/3,P2)
/!ps_r_qbezier {
  /y2 exch def /x2 exch def  /y1 exch def /x1 exch def
  /y0 exch def /x0 exch def
  x0 y0
  x0 x1 add x1 add 3 div
  y0 y1 add y1 add 3 div
  x2 x1 add x1 add 3 div
  y2 y1 add y1 add 3 div
  x2 y2
  !ps_r_cbezier
} def

% Restricted cubic Bezier loop.
% HL b alpha r1 r0 x0 y0 on the stack.
% Draw a loop outside the disk of radius r0 centered on (x0,y0).
% r1 is the radius of the loop.
% alpha is the angle in degree defining direction of the loop.
% b is a boolean which is true if an arrowhead is requested
% HL is only present when b is true and define the length of the arrowhead.
%
% The loop is defined by a cubic Bezier curve with control points
% (x0,y0) (x0+4*(r0+2*r1)/3,y0+2*sqrt(3)*r1)
% (x0+4*(r0+2*r1)/3,y0-2*sqrt(3)*r1) (x0,y0).
/!ps_r_loop {
  translate
  /r0 exch def /r1 exch def
  rotate
  r0 r0
%   Control points of the Bezier curve
  0 0
  4 r0 r1 add r1 add mul 3 div dup
  2 3 sqrt mul r1 mul dup
  3 1 roll
  neg
  0 0
  !ps_r_cbezier
} def
